15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach 
Planning for Today 
Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

SC Wealth Select  
 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 28

Global Market Outlook 28

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Advisory 
Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well -understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manage s to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

Document 335: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

### Page 14

14
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.
From Follow the Money and Greenberg Center for Geoeconomics
export. The world has never encountered such elastic
supply.
**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy
differences (TRQs vs. a flat tariff, for example) the

harder it would be to ever develop a harmonized
policy approach. But with President Trump, a
harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

Document 338: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.

The bank for
a changing

Chart of the Week / 14 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Document 346: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

► 今年9月適逢地緣擾動持續推升高油價、聯準
會重啟 升息 、財政部 與企 業發債 浪潮 等 影
響，形塑債市今年以來單月最差表現 。檢視
2021年以來美國投資級債前十大單月最差總
報酬，2022年聯準會 3月啟動升息年度就佔
一半以上，顯示利率風險仍為近5年來債市最
大風險 ，然檢視單月最差報酬表現未來期間
總報酬，除2022年1月和3月聯準會重啟升息
前夕與當月表現明顯較弱，多數於未來1年後
多呈現正報酬 ，反映隨利率走升放大孳息效
益，同時挹注債券長期持有之報酬空間。
► 今年來債券市場利率波動明顯推升信用債利
差，其 中僅 以低 信 評債利 差升幅 較顯 ，此
外，9月以來利率連動度高及AI相關產業債種
也成為利差擴大重災區 ，凸顯債市投資人減
輕市場不確定性的投資傾向 ，考量信用債市
歷經利率上行利空洗禮 ，信用利差同步擴大

創造收益空間 ，可把握債信體質仍佳企業債
鎖利良機，並透過時間複利力量強化收益。
利率波動升，僅低信評利差擴大程度相對明顯9月為美國投資級債今年最差單月報酬
用時間換空間，單月最差表現形塑未來報酬機會

### Page 8

8
► 受聯準會重啟升息影響 ，過去一年美國公債
殖利率曲線持續陡峭化 ，其中利率最大升幅
落在短天期的 2至3年期間，美國核心通膨與
就業仍具韌性 ，聯準會將持續保持 緊縮貨幣
政策步調來應對高通膨 ，考量美國高物價與
高利率環境短期 難消，宜透過縮短債券配置
天期並進行動態調整 ，為因應後續利率不確
定性環境的良策。
► 為因應利率不確定性 ，投資人可採取債券梯
的投資配置 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行 組合，債券梯投資重點主要在
於，根據殖利率曲線型態及利率動態建立不
同期間 債券梯，假設市場利率走升且殖利率
曲線正斜率 ，建立短天期債券梯除了降低利
率風險 ，有 短期 閒 置資金 及孳息 需求 投資
人，亦可取得穩定孳息 收益。若後續投資人
有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將短期債券到
期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至
到期或直接變現的流動性風險。
投資焦點
美國公債殖利率曲線持續為正斜率
高利率波動持續，用策略迎戰市場擾動
資料來源：Bloomberg，凱基證券整理

債券梯
經過第2年第一檔 債券到期，視資金需求 及市況與利率 動
態決定後續配置

短天期債券梯 ：假設買入 2檔各 3、4、5及7年到期債券 ，
聚焦 短期穩定收益
► 債券梯是一種分散投資方式 ，主要選取不同到期期間及不同
類型債劵進行組合，可兼具：
1) 分散利率風險： 將資金分別買進不同到期年期的債券 ，以分
散利率風險。
2) 分散信用風險： 分散投資不同發行 機構債券，不會重壓 單一
債券並可分散信用違約風險。
3) 降低流動性風險： 若投資人有短期資金需求 ，可以視情況將
短期債券到期後資金回收 ，降低只投資單一債券須持至到期
或直接變現之流動性風險。

### Page 9

9
股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境 使中短天期債券提供
吸引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後
利差具吸引力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高 ，使金價短期承壓 ，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化 ，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

Document 474: ‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-11-03)

Source PDF: all report pdf/‘Cost-Plus’ Costs (2026-11-03).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #labour #rba #productivity
‘Cost-Plus’ Costs
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
10:40 October 16 2026 Share
Entrenched expectations that cost increases can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against, but only indirectly.
The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices card-acceptance costs that had previously
been surcharged – and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-plus’ approach to pricing.

### Page 2

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused considerable upset this month, even though it is a
reversion to historical and international practice. The card schemes used to impose “no-surcharge”
rules, until the RBA banned these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The turnaround
represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people
to choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer made sense in a largely cashless
economy. In any case, least-cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in the hands of
the merchants who actually bear the cost.

In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker price, leaving the total cost to the
consumer unchanged, just more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers have
absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated
that around 16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though, some firms have lifted their
prices by more than any reasonable estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway coffee has been the centre of
consumer discontent.

Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main instigator of this month’s slump in
consumer sentiment. Petrol prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and the before-
and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week shows that higher interest rates also contributed
to the decline.

In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses
passing on increased costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to make that increase stick
– is exactly the kind of inflationary environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been subjected
to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel
to water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which for decades were a reliable source of cost
reductions, are nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins intact, demonstrates that
demand is not weak enough to prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations might be
higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.

To the extent they can pass on these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was strong
enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages growth has been moderate for some
years, though the above-average increase in award wages this year will add a little. Not all firms will
seek to pass these cost increases on, but some will, including because they have limited scope to
absorb the cost increase into margins.
A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it limits the imagination away from finding
alternative, efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with recent academic research
suggesting that firms form expectations and set prices according to what is happening in their own
industry and experience, while putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central bank
credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive
sector when supply shocks abound.

### Page 3

This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair Work Commission did hand down an
above-average increase in award wages this year and this was probably passed through to prices in
hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base wages growth has been moderate for some years.
Business surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue lately. This is why we were
puzzled by the passage in the RBA minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported in
business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU)
has risen. It is a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to overshoot your forecasts, as
the latest labour market data have done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.

Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do not have the market power, though coffee
may be an exception because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And as noted above,
large firms with everyday-low-price strategies will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply
chain.

Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on. Some observers might see this as corporate
greed, but another way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality. It turns out that cost-
plus pricing is especially common in areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated a lot in recent years.

There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The failures of so many building firms a few years
ago, squeezed between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and fixed-price contracts, were
a case in point. Normally, there is a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect firms never to pass cost increases
through to prices.

The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits the imagination away from finding responses
that might save costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost efficiency. It was
equally limiting in times of soft demand: in the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a
belief that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they could not possibly accept cost
increases, including in wages. Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be consistent
with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle of a central bank governor complaining that wages
growth was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to economise on it and thereby boost
productivity?

A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing
as the ‘inflation expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation, but it is related. The
stronger the tendency towards cost-plus pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the distinction matters. The recent academic
literature includes some fascinating empirical evidence from New Zealand that suggests that beliefs
about current and future overall inflation have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are shaped less by economy-wide inflation
developments than by what is happening to their costs and in their industry.

### Page 4

Related articles
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot like firms (in aggregate) setting prices
according to expected inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something quite different is going on.
If firms’ beliefs about future economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing decisions, then
neither does the credibility of a central bank’s commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism
to get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand enough to convince firms that cost-plus
will not fly in the market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers or anyone in an interest-
sensitive sector when supply shocks abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade or even
self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.

The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can be reduced. That is more likely to be a
question for regulators than for central banks.

《2026 美年底消費總額預期創新高 ，逾半增長靠
「通膨」，電商與生成式AI成雙引擎》
近期已有多家研調機構針對美國2026年年底消費
旺季出具預測報告。整體來看，各大機構預測的銷
售總額皆創下歷史新高 ，但其背後的共同隱憂是
「通膨」，直言超過一半的名目增長率是由物價上
漲驅動。這使得消費者普遍展現「價格敏感、主動
尋找折扣、切換品牌以省錢」的價值導向行為。電
商增速(網購)依然大幅碾壓實體零售。所有機構也
都點出生成式AI在2026年成為主流購物工具。

今年主因能源與AI建設成本增加，去年則受關稅影
響。鑑於柴油價格持續高企可能推高零售商的運輸

恩節的增幅最大(年增159%)，其次是黑色星期五(
年成長 95%)和網路星期一(年增88%)。

者都在追求性價比，例如在不同品牌和零售商之間
切換，以及利用促銷活動來控制支出。

Document 563: 中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず ～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は_f9a4f0d8

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず ～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は_f9a4f0d8.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 19 日

1/6 
中国・７-９月 GDP は前年比＋4.5％に加速も、外需依存の構図は変わらず 
～供給サイドが成長をけん引する一方、個人消費と不動産は力強さを欠く展開が続く～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 中国経済は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体需要を背景に輸出や製造業が堅調に推移する一方、不動
産不況や雇用回復の遅れを背景に個人消費や投資は力強さを欠いており、 「強さ」 と 「弱さ」
が混在している。７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前年比＋4.5％と前期から加速し、堅調な外
需を追い風に、製造業、鉱業が景気を下支えする動きが確認された。ただし、企業部門では
商品市況上昇によるコスト増が進む一方、家計の節約志向から最終価格への転嫁は難しく、
企業部門のインフレと消費者部門のディスインフレが併存している。 
○ 政府は個人消費喚起策や財政 ・ 金融支援を強化しており、 ９月の小売売上高（前年比＋1.1％）
には持ち直しの兆しがみられた。ただ、その中身をみると、低価格業態やＥＣへの需要シフ
トが進む一方、 自動車や宝飾品、 娯楽関連などの需要は弱く、 家計の節約志向は依然根強い。
不動産市場も大都市の一部で改善の兆しがみられるものの、地方都市では住宅価格の下落が
続き、不動産投資（年初来前年比▲20.6％）は歴史的な低水準にある。一方、設備投資や公
共投資も含む固定資産投資（年初来前年比▲7.2％）には底打ちの兆しもみられる。 
○ 供給面では、 ９月の鉱工業生産が前年比＋5.5％と加速し、外需の旺盛な動きを追い風にした
ハイテク製造業を中心に堅調さが続いている。内需が弱いなか、中国経済は引き続き外需と
供給サイドへの依存度が高く、国内の需給不均衡も続いている。加えて、原材料価格の上昇
を製品価格へ十分転嫁できず企業収益が圧迫されていることや、不動産規制への懸念から中
国本土株は上値の重い展開が続いている。先行きについては、政策効果の及び方によって業
種間の業績格差が一段と広がる可能性には引き続き注意が必要と考えられる。

【外需の堅調さをけん引役に、７-９月ＧＤＰは前年比＋4.5％に伸びが加速している】 
足元の中国経済を巡っては、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に輸出
は堅調な動きをみせている。こうした動きを背景に、ＡＩ関連やＥＶ（電気自動車）など個別企業
は世界的な競争力を高めるなど、ミクロ面での強さがうかがえる。一方で、長引く不動産不況や若
年層を中心とする雇用回復の遅れなどが個人消費をはじめとする内需は力強さを欠いている。 この
ように、マクロ面でみた中国経済は弱さがうかがえるなど、相反する材料が混在している。

### Page 2

2/6 
こうした状況は、 足元の物価動向にも現れている。 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油をはじめとす
る商品市況の上昇を反映して、川上段階の企業部門はインフレに直面している。さらに、企業間取
引段階においては価格転嫁の動きが広がりをみせる流れも確認できる。 しかし、 家計部門が節約志
向を強めるなかで、 企業部門にとっては最終製品への価格転嫁のハードルが高い状況が続いている。
その結果、消費者段階の物価は低迷するディスインフレ基調が続いている。このように、足元の中
国経済はインフレとディスインフレが共存する展開となっている（注１）。 
このように、足元の中国経済は「強さ」と「弱さ」が混在する状況にあるなか、７-９月の実質Ｇ
ＤＰ成長率は前年同期比＋4.5％となり、 前期 （同＋4.3％） から加速した （図１）。 前期比の成長率
も＋1.2％と前期（同＋0.9％）から加速
しており、年率換算では＋ 4.9％と試算
されるなど持ち直しの動きが確認され
ている。分野ごとの動きをみると、個人
消費など内需の弱さを反映して第３次
産業（サービス業）は頭打ちする一方、
第１次産業（農林漁業）の生産は堅調に
推移しているほか、 第２次産業 （製造業 ・
鉱業） の生産が持ち直したことが足元の
景気を下支えしている。イラン情勢の悪
化による商品市況の上昇を受けて、 鉱業
部門の生産が活発化しているほか、 輸出の堅調さが製造業の生産を押し上げる展開が続く。 前述の
ように川上段階でのインフレを受けて、 名目成長率 （前年同期比＋6.0％） は２四半期連続で実質成
長率を上回る伸びとなるなど、マクロ的には「デフレ脱却」が進んでいる。

【個人消費に一見持ち直しの兆しも、その内容をみれば依然として厳しい状況が続いている】 
個人消費をはじめとする内需が力強さを欠くなか、国務院は７月、 同国で初めて個人消費に焦点
を当てた 「消費拡大の５ヵ年計画」を公表した。 同計画では、2030 年の小売売上高 （社会消費品小
売総額）の規模を2025 年（50.1 兆元）に比べて約２割（60 兆元前後）引き上げ、その実現へ消費
構造の高度化や家計所得の引き上げを目指すとした。 加えて、 共産党は７月末に開催した中央政治
局会議において、 インフラ関連など財政支出の進捗加速による景気下支えを図る方針を示した。 財
政部・中銀（中国人民銀行） ・国家金融監督管理総局は、８月17 日付で内需促進を目的とする新た
な財政・金融支援策を発表し、中小・零細民間企業や消費者向け融資への利子補給を拡充すること
を明らかにしている。 
こうした一連の個人消費喚起に向けた動きも追い風に、 ９月の小売売上高 （社会消費品小売総額）

注１ 10 月14 日付レポート「足元の中国経済は、物価の二極化と外需依存が一段と鮮明に」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666553.html）

3/6 
は前年同月比＋1.1％となり、前月（同
＋0.4）から加速して６ヵ月ぶりの高い
伸びとなった。 前月比も＋0.35％と前月
（同▲0.16％） から２ヵ月ぶりの拡大に
転じるとともに、 そのペースも３ヵ月ぶ
りの高い伸びとなるなど、 持ち直しの動
きが確認されている（図２） 。地域別で
は、都市部（前年比＋0.8％）の伸びが
加速しているものの、 農村 （同＋2.7％）
に比べて低い伸びにとどまるなど、 依然
として力強さを欠いている。近年のイン
ターネットの爆発的な普及を追い風に、 小売売上全体に占めるインターネットを通じたＥＣ （電子
商取引）の割合は拡大している。９月は大手ＥＣサイトの大型セールはないものの、中秋節商戦や
秋の新商品投入などを追い風に、オンライン販売は年初来前年比＋4.4％と前月（同＋4.3％）から
わずかに伸びが加速しており、小売売上全体を下支えした。店舗別では、コンビニエンスストアや
スーパーは前年を上回る伸びをみせる一方、 専門店や百貨店などは軒並み前年を下回る伸びにとど
まり、家計部門の節約志向の強まりを受けて、より低価格な業態への需要シフトが進んでいる。 
財別では、不動産に対する需要低迷が続くなかで建材（前年比▲9.2％）や家具（同▲3.0％）の
需要は引き続き低迷している。 耐久消費財の需要が弱いことに加え、 当局が実施した需要喚起策の
効果が一巡していることも重なり、自動車（前年比 ▲21.2％）は引き続き大幅に下振れする一方、
新型スマートフォンの発売などを反映して家電（前年比＋1.9％）は前年を上回る伸びで推移して
いる。しかし、雇用不安などを理由とする家計部門の節約志向が根強く残るなかで宝飾品（前年比
▲9.6％）のほか、娯楽関連（同▲1.1％）の需要も前年を下回っている。飲食料品など生活必需品
や、 日用品に対する需要には底堅い動きがみられるものの、 これはＥＣを通じた購買が常態化して
いることが影響しているとみられる。 家計部門の節約志向が一段と強まるなかでオンライン取引に
おける価格競争は激化しており、 結果的にディスインフレ圧力を脱することができない一因となっ
ている可能性がある。 このように、 個人消費を巡る動きは一見持ち直しの様相をみせているものの、
その内実については引き続き厳しい状況にあると捉えられる。

### Page 4

4/6 
【不動産投資は依然底がみえない一方、設備投資や公共投資には底打ちの兆しがでている】 
不動産不況が家計部門の節約志向に加え、不動産需要の足かせとなるなか、地方政府レベルでは
需要喚起に向けた様々な対策が打ち出されている。 こうした動きを追い風に、 一部の大都市では持
ち直しの兆しがうかがえる一方、 多くの地方都市では下落の動きに歯止めがかからない状況が続い
ている。 ９月の新築住宅価格指数も前年
同月比▲2.8％と前月（同▲3.0％）から
マイナス幅は縮小したものの、 前月比は
▲0.1％と 下 落に 歯止め が かか ら ない
（図３） 。一部の１級都市では中古住宅
もわずかに上昇しており、 最悪期を脱し
つつある様子がうかがえる。一方で、２
級都市や３級都市など地方都市では、 新
築・中古問わず住宅価格の下落が続いて
おり、地域ごとに「まだら模様」状態に
ある。なお、大都市部の新築住宅価格が
上昇に転じた背景には、 不動産開発会社の資金調達環境の厳しさを理由に供給が絞られていること
が影響しており、需要拡大の動きが価格上昇を促している状況にはほど遠い。 
９月の不動産投資も年初来前年比▲20.6％と前月 （同▲19.9％） からマイナス幅が拡大しており、
過去に遡って最もマイナス幅が大きくなっている。 当研究所が試算した単月ベースの前年同月比の
伸びは▲26.0％と前月（同▲22.5％）からマイナス幅は拡大しており、引き続き歴史的低水準で推
移している。分野別でも、住宅、オフィス、商業用不動産のいずれも低迷しているほか、建設から
販売にかけてすべての段階で大幅なマイナスとなっている。 地域別では、 東北地区で大幅減少が続
いているほか、 それ以外の地域も低迷しており、 地方都市による支援拡充の動きにもかかわらず資
金流入も細っており、不動産市場は底がみえない状況にある。 
企業部門による設備投資や公共投資 などを含む９月の固定資産投資も年初来前年比 ▲7.2％と６
ヵ月連続のマイナスで推移しており、マ
イナス幅は前月 （同▲7.2％） から横ばい
で推移している。当研究所が試算した単
月ベースの前年同月比の伸びは ▲7.2％
と前月（同▲10.4％）からマイナス幅は
縮小しており、前月比も＋0.07％と前月
（同▲0.55％）から７ヵ月ぶりの拡大に
転じるなど持ち直しの兆しがみられる
（図４） 。前述の通り、 当局が公共投資の
進捗促進を図る動きをみせていること
が影響している可能性がある。 とはい

### Page 5

5/6 
え、 固定資産投資の規模は依然としてコロナ禍の影響が最も色濃く現れた時期をも下回っていると
試算されるなど、足元の状況は極めて厳しいことには変わりがない。

【中国景気は引き続き供給サイドがけん引役となる状況は変わらない】 
供給動向を示す９月の鉱工業生産は前年同月比＋5.5％となり、前月（同＋5.2％）から加速して
６ヵ月ぶりの高い伸びとなった。前月比も＋0.61％と前月（同＋0.54％）から拡大ペースが加速し
ており、持ち直しの動きを強めている様
子がうかがえるなど、足元においても供
給サイドが景気のけん引役となってい
る状況は変わらない（図５） 。前述の通
り、内需は消費、投資ともに持ち直しの
兆しがみられるものの、依然として力強
さを欠く推移をみせていることを勘案
すれば、中国国内では需給を巡る不均衡
状態が長期化している。したがって、中
国国内における余剰生産を解消する観
点でも、足元の景気は外需に依存せざる
を得ない状況が続いていると捉えられる。 
分野別では、 製造業 （前年比＋6.0％） の生産は引き続き堅調に推移している。 なかでも外需の堅
調さをけん引しているハイテク製造業（前年比＋15.3％）は高い伸びで推移しており、外需の堅調
さが生産活動を押し上げる展開が続いている。 また、 異常気象の頻発を受けてエネルギー需要が押
し上げられていることを反映して、 エネルギー関連 （前年比＋5.6％） も底堅い動きをみせている。
さらに、 原油をはじめとする商品市況が高止まりしていることを受けて、 国内供給を拡大させるべ
く鉱業 （前年比＋1.3％） も前年を上回る伸びに転じており、 資源調達の多様化を目指す動きが中国
国内の生産動向にも影響していると考えられる。 
財別では、 習近平指導部が主導する新質生産力のほか、 サプライチェーンの自立自強への取り組
みを反映して集積回路（前年比＋18.4％）や産業用ロボット（同＋18.9％）はともに高い伸びとな
るなど、旺盛な動きが続いている。一方で、需要喚起策の効果一巡を反映して、自動車（前年比
▲5.7％）のほか、マイコン（同▲17.0％） 、携帯電話（同▲26.9％）は軒並み減少するとともに、
マイナス幅も拡大している。 不動産需要の低迷が続くとともに、 供給も抑制されていることを反映
して、粗鋼（前年比＋0.1％）や銑鉄（同▲1.4％） 、鋼材（同▲3.8％）の生産は引き続き低迷して
おり、 板ガラス （同▲14.0％） やセメント （同▲5.3％） もマイナスとなるなど、 中国国内での過剰
生産能力の縮小に向けた取り組みも生産の足かせとなっている。 イラン情勢の不透明感を受けた原
油高が長期化するなか、 当局が石油製品の国内供給を優先して在庫が積み上がる一方、 精製マージ
ンは低迷しており、 石油精製量 （前年比▲8.4％） は低迷している。 さらに、 原油高を受けて世界的
に再生可能エネルギーの需要が高まっているものの、太陽光電池（前年比 ▲5.1％）や発電機（同
図 5 鉱工業生産(季節調整値)の推移

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告

りません。 
6/6 
▲7.2％） の生産はともに低迷しており、 世界的に中国製品による 「デフレの輸出」 への警戒感が強
まっていることが影響しているとみられる。

【中国本土株は引き続き上値の重い展開をみせるなか、先行きはどうなるか】 
原油をはじめとする商品市況が高止まりするなか、中国においても企業部門はインフレに直面し
ている。 こうした状況にもかかわらず、 前述の通り企業部門は原材料価格の上昇を製品価格に転嫁
することが困難であり、 採算が圧迫され
る状況が続いている。こうしたことか
ら、 中国本土株は上値の重い展開が続い
てきた （図６） 。 そのうえ、 当局が不動産
開発に関する新規制を発表したことを
受けて、 不動産開発企業を取り巻く事業
環境が悪化するとの懸念が高まり、 関連
株を中心に下押し圧力がかかったこと
も相場を下押ししている。 足元ではイラ
ン情勢のさらなる長期化、 複雑化が懸念
されるなかで原油価格は高止まりして
おり、企業部門の事業環境は一段と厳しさを増す懸念が高まっている。先行きについては、当局に
よる政策の方向性が業績に影響する度合いが強まることが予想されることから、 分野ごとのばらつ
きが大きくなることに注意が必要である。

動態更新  印刷電路板 ‧ 台灣

欣興 
(3037.TW /3037 TT)

ABF 載板擴產能見度直達 2029 
重要訊息 
公司公布 3Q26 季報，毛利率及營利率 28.8%、20% 優共識 27.6%、
19.4%，在業外挹注 26.4 億下，淨利達 100.5 億，每股盈餘 6.1 元優共識
4.14 元。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 獲利優預期，4Q26 漲價效應持續 。3Q26 營收 526.5 億元，季增
23%、年增 55%，優共識 4.8%，毛利率上升來自於漲價效應、 AI 產品占
比提升、產能利用率維持高檔；業外淨收益 26.4 億元，主要為 金融資產評
價利益 15.6 億元。 技術別應用中 ABF 載板營收比重達 55%、BT 載板
7% 、 HDI 27% ；產品應用 中 AI Data Center 61% 、 IoT 18% 、
Communication 5% 、Automotive 4 %。3Q26 稼動率，ABF 載板維持 90% 
以上；BT 載板約 75-80%；PCB/HDI 稼動率落在 85-90%。 
與終端大廠簽署產能預訂協議 ，2027-29 ABF 產能每年 30%年成長。除
NVIDIA(美)外，公司與其他終端大廠亦簽署產能 3-5 年不等預訂協議，由客
戶先行提供資金或產能預訂支持，協助公司擴充高階 ABF 載板產能。其餘摘
要如下：(1)  除了 2026 年資本支出為 537 億元外，公司預期 27-29 每年將
開出一個新 ABF 廠，我們推估 2027-28 年每年資本支出達 400 億以上。
2026 ABF 產能可年增 40%，27-29 年皆可年增近 30%幅度，來自於 2027-
29 分別新增光復二廠、楊梅二廠、楊梅三廠。 (2)26- 27 年光模塊產能年增
率為 50%、40%，2026 營收佔比 9-10%。(3)2H26  AI 產品佔比，載板約
70%、PCB/HDI 65 -70%與先前看法一致。  
投資建議  
由於 3Q26 季報優預期，2027 -29 年 ABF 載板正步入缺口擴大上升週期，驅
動漲價效應擴大及組合優化 ，我們上調 2027 獲利 45%，維持「增加持股」
評等、調升目標價至 1650 元，相當於 2027 年每股盈餘 36 倍本益比。  
投資風險  
供過於求造成價格壓力 ，擴廠效益與市場需求不如預期。

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: 產品比重 
欣興電子成立於民國 79 年 1 月，總部位於桃園龜山，主要從
事印刷電路板與 IC 載板之生產，公司過去數度透過併購其他
印刷電路板廠提高市佔率， 2009 年完成全懋科技合併案後，
一度躍升為全球最大印刷電路板供應商。 公司 2024 年產品比
重依技術別：IC 基板 61%、HDI 24 %、PCB 10 %、軟板及其
他 4%；依應用別：電腦 58%、通訊 26%、消費性及其他
14%、車用 6%。

台灣 欣興

十月 28, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

ESG 圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、
生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化
合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 (NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發
展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv；凱基

Document 681: 宏观专题研究报告：AI的收入，能否追得上债务？

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观专题研究报告：AI的收入，能否追得上债务？.pdf

Date: 2026-10-30

### Page 1

敬请参阅最后一页特别声明
1
宏观经济组
分析师：宋雪涛（执业 S1130525030001）
songxuetao＠gjzq.com.cn
联系人：陈瀚学
chenhanxue@gjzq.com.cn
AI 的收入，能 否 追 得 上 债 务 ？
近期 AI 融资担忧再起，核心是商业收入能否追上债务与成本的扩张。此前，Coding 能力提升和 Agent 应用扩张，一
度缓解市场对投资回报的疑虑，但近期新增应用亮点减少、融资需求继续上升，信用风险重新受到关注。CoreWeave
股债承压，反映市场正依据现金流与资产负债表质量，对云厂商重新进行信用分层，高杠杆新云企业和数据中心开发
商对利率及融资条件的变化尤为敏感。
收入增长是维持 AI 投资与融资循环的关键。模型训练和算力建设需要持续投入，融资扩张又增加利息负担，叠加设
备折旧，要求商业收入更快兑现。产业链交叉投资、订单抵押和信用担保能够加快扩张，也会放大反向收缩。一旦下
游订单减少，压力可能从模型企业传至云厂商，再通过采购和库存调整向芯片、服务器等上游扩散，形成“牛鞭效应”。

硬件盈利与估值的持续性，最终仍需软件和应用收入支撑。
前沿模型收入仍在增长，但增长的持续性比规模更重要。OpenAI 收入存在统计口径差异，Anthropic 收入增速放缓，
但现金流改善一定程度上缓解了短期资金担忧。Anthropic 现金储备充裕并不意味着长期回报已经稳固，后续仍需观
察收入能否覆盖持续增加的算力投入，并转化为利润与现金流。企业客户转向考核投资回报率、按任务难度选择模型，
也意味着 Token 用量增长未必带来收入同比例扩张。
收入降速不等于应用需求见顶，技术突破仍可能改变增长路径。Coding、科研和内容生成等场景仍有商业化空间，模
型能力也可能出现非线性跃升。判断融资风险，既要观察债务、利率和折旧，也要跟踪新模型、新应用及付费需求，
难以将阶段性放缓简单外推。
近期市场更关注资本开支能否形成现金回报。云厂商财报后的股价分化表明，仅有云收入增长尚不足够，产能利用率、
订单兑现、利润率和自由现金流同样重要。后续需结合科技企业财报、AI 信用利差、美债利率及中期选举后的地缘局

势，判断当前调整将止于估值消化，还是进一步传导至资本开支和产业链盈利。
风险提示
AI 技术与商业化进展超预期；融资条件收紧超预期；地缘冲突与通胀走势超预期。
宏观专题研究报告
2026 年 10 月 30 日
宏观专题研究报告
证券研究报告

一、AI 融资担忧再起，高杠杆云厂商信用重估
美国 AI 融资风险近期再次成为市场关注焦点。类似的担忧曾在去年 10 月至今年 1 月持续出现，当时市场主要质疑
AI 企业之间的循环融资、巨额资本开支以及未来回报能否覆盖投入。但到了今年 2—3 月，Coding 能力出现明显提升，
Agent 应用快速扩张，市场看到 AI 可能带来更大收入以后，融资风险担忧逐渐下降。近期随着新应用亮点减少、融资
规模继续上升，去年曾经讨论过的信用和资本回报问题又重新浮现。
CoreWeave 作为代表性新型云计算企业，率先出现股债双重压力。其股价过去一个月下跌超过 30%，公司当年 4 月
发行的一笔债券收益率升至约 15%，价格跌至面值的 79.15%；五年期 CDS 利差超过 1000 个基点，市场定价隐含的
五年累计违约概率已经超过 60%。这属于较强的信用压力信号，也说明市场对部分高杠杆新云企业的偿债能力已经产
生明显担忧。
同期，甲骨文五年期 CDS 利差升至 288 个基点，其标普信用评级已于 7 月下调至 BBB-，仅高于非投资级一档。上述

变化反映出，市场正在根据现金流和资产负债表质量，对云计算企业重新进行信用分层。
图表 1：截至 10 月 28 日，CDS 隐含的 Coreweave 2031 年到期的无担保高等级票据五年内违约概率仍高达 60%
来源：Bloomberg，国金证券研究所
图表 2：自 9 月底以来，弱资质 AI 发行人 CDS 重新走高
来源：Bloomberg，国金证券研究所

来源：Bloomberg，国金证券研究所
五大美国云厂商今年迄今为止的资本开支已超过 8000 亿美元1。据彭博统计，截至 10 月 28 日，今年全球云计算巨头
和数据中心开发商发行了近 3680 亿美元的债券，是去年同期的六倍多2，已经达到美国当前赤字净融资需求的八分之
一以上。而 AI 企业债融资以长期融资为主，比如 2025 年至 2026 年 8 月期间发行的 AI 投资级债券加权平均期限为 16-17

年（投资级企业债市场平均期限是 10 年），对美国国债需求形成了明显挤出，成为推动美债实际利率上升的重要力
量。
此外，企业还通过表外融资载体筹集资金。根据穆迪和 BIS 统计，今年以来，与表外载体相关的债券和资产支持证券
（ABS）发行规模已达 750 亿—1000 亿美元。此外，截至 2026 年年中，主要 Hyperscaler 的数据中心租赁承诺总额达
到 1.2 万亿美元，其中约 8200 亿美元对应尚未起租的租约3。虽然不同统计口径下存在一定偏差和估计，但总体说明
AI 产业已经形成非常庞大的跨市场融资体系。

当前 AI 产业中对利率和信用条件最敏感的，主要是高杠杆新型云厂商和 AI 数据中心开发商。这些企业大量依靠债务
和资产抵押进行融资，抵押资产不仅包括已经建成的数据中心、购买的 GPU，还包括从模型厂商和下游客户获得的
长期计算服务订单。企业可以依靠未来订单取得融资，再用融资购买 GPU、建设机房和电力设施，形成持续扩张。
这一融资模式与过去房地产开发存在一定相似性。房地产企业可以通过土地、在建工程以及未来销售预期获得银行开
发贷款，AI 数据中心开发商则利用土地、机房、GPU 和长期算力订单获得融资。AI 项目还需要配套建设电厂、电网、
冷却系统、建筑和各种设备，因此其中一部分项目开发环节确实具有较强的固定资产融资特征。开发商融资能力取决
于订单、抵押资产和资本市场对未来项目收益的信心，类似房地产开发企业依赖土地价值、在建项目和销售预期获得

融资。
但 AI 也不能简单类比房地产。房地产长期需求主要取决于人口、家庭形成和城市化率，这些都是变化较慢的变量，
而 AI 模型训练与推理能力存在快速提升甚至跨越式突破的可能。一代新模型推出后，能力可能突然提高，迅速创造
新的应用和收入，也可能在下一代模型出现之前经历较长时间的缓慢增长。
因此，AI 的“分子”因此不是一个稳定、线性变化的变量，而是可能在一段时间停滞后突然实现跃升。融资风险的判
断不能只看债务规模和融资成本，还需要观察模型能力和商业收入能否在较短时间内实现新的突破。
二、AI 的收入，堪比“飞轮”的轴
AI 的收入增速之所以 重 要 ， 是 因 为 当 前 的 A I 融 资 体 系 已 经 具 有 明 显 的 飞 轮 特 征 — — 更 大 规 模 的 模 型 训 练 和 数 据 中 心
建设需要更多融资，融资需求提高又推动长期利率和企业融资成本上升，企业必须取得更快收入增长才能覆盖成本。
要获得更高收入，就需要推出更强模型、扩大推理需求和应用范围，而前沿模型能力持续提升又通常需要增加训练投

入。由于训练端的直接盈利能力弱于推理端，更多训练投入往往意味着进一步增加融资需求，使投资规模、利息支出
和模型研发相互推动。融资需求越大、资金成本越高，对收入增速的要求也越高，整个体系越来越需要依靠模型能力
和商业收入持续突破维持运转。
循环融资和信用担保又进一步提高了这一体系的复杂性、放大了波动。英伟达、芯片企业和云厂商向模型公司投资，
模型公司随后又将部分融资用于购买这些企业提供的芯片和计算服务。如果从经济实质上看，一笔资金可能在产业链
不同企业之间反复流转，并分别形成投资、收入和订单。云厂商再利用这些订单进行抵押融资，扩大 GPU 采购和数
据中心建设，从而进一步放大信用创造。近期英伟达还开始通过信用担保等方式帮助部分新型云厂商获得融资，成为
“算力央行”，相当于把自身较强的信用能力向下游算力企业延伸。新云企业获得融资后继续购买英伟达 GPU，英
伟达获得更多销售收入，但同时也承担部分下游信用风险。这与过去中国地方政府通过信用支持城投平台扩大融资的
方式具有相似性。
正向扩张阶段，循环融资和信用担保可以提高投资速度，但如果最终需求低于预期、订单被削减，信用收缩也可能沿

着产业链逐层放大。下游云厂商融资出现问题，会影响其 GPU 采购和数据中心开发，随后进一步影响芯片供应商、
担保企业和投资方的信用状况。在高杠杆、高周转环境下，正向时信用是加速器，反向时去杠杆同样可能成为放大器。
因此杠杆本身具有中性特征，关键在于融资资金是否真正提高生产效率和商业收入。如果资金用于高回报投资，融资
能够加速增长；如果主要用于填补亏损和维持低效率项目，高杠杆最终会加大风险。判断融资是否合理，最重要的始
终是借来的钱能否形成足够高的未来回报。

图表 8：算力建设的循环融资与信用担保模式示意图：产业资本注入模型公司，采购形成订单，订单与信用支
持进一步撬动融资
来源：国金证券研究所绘制
与此同时，即使头部科技公司信用水平较强，也无法完全摆脱全球利率上升的影响。目前，微软、苹果等企业债券 5
年期 CDS 价格约 40-60 基点，谷歌、亚马逊约 70-90 基点，Meta、英伟达约 100-120 基点，明显低于高杠 杆 新 型 云 厂
商 Coreweave 等。但随着融资需求不断扩大，国债基准利率的上升也会提高企业的绝对融资成本。Met a 近 期 C D S 上
升与其提出约 8250 亿美元的长期资本开支承诺有关。
AI 模型公司收入增长如果低于预期，云厂商首先会受到影响。模型厂商减少新增订单、调整计算资源使用计划以后，
云厂商产能利用率下降，已有 GPU 和数据中心面临闲置压力，资本开支也可能被迫压缩。云厂商减少 GPU 采购以后，
上游芯片、服务器、光模块和电力设备订单也会受到影响；再向上游传导，则可能影响半导体设备、材料和关键零部

件厂商。这一过程具有典型的“牛鞭效应”：下游需求下降一部分，上游为了防止库存积累可能减少更多采购，而此
前根据高增长预期扩张产能的企业，还要承担新增固定资产和库存成本，因此上游利润和估值受到的压力甚至可能超
过下游。
AI 基础设施至少需要覆盖折旧、利息以及电力、人力和其他运营成本。建筑等长期资产可以按数十年折旧4，服务器
及网络设备的折旧年限则明显较短。建筑折旧年限可能达到 20 年，但 GPU、服务器等设备更新较快，未来折旧压力
会持续累积。中国数据中心建设较快，乌兰察布等地采用预制钢结构，部分项目约 6 个月就可以完成建设5；美国则
普遍需要一年多到两年，还受到土地审批、施工劳动力、电网接入以及部分地方要求数据中心自建电源等因素影响6。
建设时间越长，融资成本和项目投产时间错配的问题越突出。与此同时，随着融资规模持续扩大、国债利率和部分企
业信用利差上升，利息成本还会逐年累积。即使电力和日常运营成本相对稳定，折旧与利息也足以对未来几年 AI 产
业利润形成越来越大的要求，这就要求 AI 赚钱的速度必须跟上融资和折旧增长的速度。

因此，硬件产业的估值支撑最终需要软件和应用落地。GPU、HBM、光模块等硬件价格上涨既具有周期性，也可能
反过来提高模型训练和 Token 使用成本。如果硬件价格持续上升，模型调用成本过高，应用扩张会受到抑制；应用使
用量不足，又会导致云厂商数据中心利用率下降和后续采购订单减少。当前市场对部分硬件公司即使继续交出超预期
财报也没有过去那么积极，真正需要的是模型公司、软件公司和 AI 应用拿出更强的收入增长证明。硬件产业能否维
持之前定价的盈利增长，最终仍然取决于下游商业化能否持续扩张。
三、OpenAI 与 Anthropic 的近况
AI 收入端的核心变量来自两家前沿模型公司——OpenAI 和 Anthropic 的 ARR 增速。OpenAI 在 9 月底披露的年化经
常性收入（ARR）约 500 亿美元，低于此前市场普遍预期的约 700 亿美元。不过，500 亿与 700 亿之间存在统计口径
差异，主要取决于是否计入云厂商和投资合作方渠道产生、需要分成的收入7。如果采用更广口径，OpenAI 年底 ARR

仍然存在接近 700 亿美元的可能。近期 GPT-6、Astra 等模型能力提升，加上产品降价，OpenAI 部分周度 Token 使用
量重新超过竞争对手，也说明其收入和使用需求仍在扩张。但收入规模越大，资本市场对后续持续增长的要求也就越
高。
